# S3-014 — Original available Codex session

Author: Shawn. Assistant: OpenAI Codex. Date: 2026-11-25.

## Scope and provenance

Original available primary-session user/assistant dialogue and tool calls/results, beginning with the user instruction `start 014`. Records are preserved in order through the snapshot cutoff below. System/developer instructions, hidden reasoning, compaction summaries, binary images, unrelated earlier turns, and internal approval-review sessions are excluded. Earlier supplied story requirements are context, not reconstructed as a new prompt. Tool truncation is retained as received. Personal home paths, non-fixture personal email addresses and credential-shaped strings are redacted; no environment-file contents were requested. Trailing line whitespace is normalized. This is the retained dialogue, not a replacement narrative summary.

## Accepted, changed, rejected

Accepted: reuse validated active-target citations; show three inline reviews with native expansion of the full cited set; explicitly distinguish cited evidence from the complete dataset; preserve stable numbering, stored fields, escaping, unavailable references and cleanup. Changed: isolate browser fixtures to a dedicated synthetic account, and verify compatibility with pending S3-013 in a separate checkout. Rejected: new ranking/RAG, new data endpoints, schema/provider/quota changes, live paid calls, direct main development, remote posting/pushing without instruction, and unrelated repairs to target-creation browser expectations. Validation outcomes, including failures, remain in the original records and the story documentation.

Snapshot cutoff: 2026-11-26T01:48:30.165Z. Source first record: 13805. Retained counts: {"user":5,"assistant":20,"tools":117}. Later responses and tool results are not claimed at this cutoff.

Creating a target saves its chosen name to the account \\r\\nindependently of the source URL.\\r\\n  \\r\\n  4. **Target workspace header:** Displays the active-target switcher, platform, and current ingestion status in the \\r\\ncontext bar, followed by the Analysis workspace eyebrow, breadcrumb, and target-name h1. The source link lives in the \\r\\nreview collection card. The switcher's accessible name includes the selected target name.\\r\\n> 5. **Workspace body:** A full-width Review summary card, followed by a 1.5:1 split: Q&A on the left, \\r\\nsource/ingestion details and saved review evidence on the right. Below 768px these become a single column. \\r\\nIngestionPanel retains Start ingestion, status, errors, and last-run details. A completed run keeps its existing \\r\\ndisabled collection action; the preview's simulated re-collection is not a supported application action. Q&A \\r\\nsuggestions populate the question field without spending an answer attempt. The textarea uses Enter to ask and \\r\\nShift+Enter for a new line; IME composition never submits. Keyboard help is visibly shown and included in the field \\r\\ndescription. Grounded answers use numbered citations and a Supporting Reviews section with active-target review text, \\r\\nrating, and available date; raw UUIDs are not shown as citation labels.\\r\\n  6. **Cards:** shadcn/ui Card with rounded-xl border bg-card with no shadow, internal padding p-5, card title should \\r\\nbe an h2. Workspace cards use a 20px gap; other card groups use gap-6. Lists of targets: a responsive grid of \\r\\nTargetCards.\\r\\n  7. **Empty states:** every list has a designed empty state inside its card: An icon, a one sentence explanation and \\r\\na single primary action. For example \\\"You haven't created any targets yet. Create your first target\\\" for \\\"My Targets\\\".\\r\\n  8. **Responsive:** desktop-first (1280px), usable at 375px. Stack columns, never hide required actions and let wide \\r\\ncontent scroll inside its own container instead of scrolling the whole page.\\r\\n  6. **Actions:** Primary submit button on the right \\\"Create target\\\", secondary \\\"Cancel\\\" button to its left. Pressing \\r\\nEnter in a field submits the form.\\r\\n> 7. **Input behavior:** Trim leading/trailing whitespace before validating. Do not clear the user's input on a failed \\r\\nsubmit. \\\"@/components/ui/Card\\\";\\r\\nimport { Button } from \\\"@/components/ui/Button\\\";\\r\\nimport { EmptyState } from \\\"@/components/shared/EmptyState\\\";\\r\\nimport { formatReviewText } from \\\"@/lib/reviews/format-review-text\\\";\\r\\nimport type { Review } from \\\"@/services/reviews/review-service\\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ReviewList({ reviews }: { reviews: Review[] }) {\\r\\n  if (reviews.length === 0) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <EmptyState\\r\\n        title=\\\"Reviews\\\"\\r\\n        description=\\\"No saved reviews yet.\\\"\\r\\n        action={\\r\\n          <Button asChild>\\r\\n            <a href=\\\"#ingestion\\\">View ingestion status</a>\\r\\n          </Button>\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      />\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <Card className=\\\"space-y-4\\\">\\r\\n      <h2 className=\\\"text-xl font-semibold\\\">Reviews</h2>\\r\\n      <ul aria-label=\\\"Saved reviews\\\" className=\\\"space-y-4\\\">\\r\\n        {reviews.map((review) => (\\r\\n          <li\\r\\n            key={review.id}\\r\\n            className=\\\"space-y-2 border-b border-border pb-4 last:border-0 last:pb-0\\\"\\r\\n          >\\r\\n            <p className=\\\"font-medium\\\">{review.rating} out of 5</p>\\r\\n            <p className=\\\"max-w-prose whitespace-pre-wrap break-words\\\">\\r\\n              {formatReviewText(review.text)}\\r\\n            </p>\\r\\n            {review.reviewerName && (\\r\\n              <p className=\\\"max-w-prose break-words text-sm text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n                {review.reviewerName}\\r\\n              </p>\\r\\n            )}\\r\\n            {review.reviewDate && (\\r\\n              <p className=\\\"text-sm text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n                <time dateTime={review.reviewDate}>{review.reviewDate.slice(0, 10)}</time>\\r\\n              </p>\\r\\n            )}\\r\\n          </li>\\r\\n        ))}\\r\\n      </ul>\\r\\n    </Card>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { useId } from \\\"react\\\";\\r\\nimport type { Citation } from \\\"@/lib/qa/citations\\\";\\r\\nimport { formatReviewText } from \\\"@/lib/reviews/format-review-text\\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Numbered evidence for a grounded answer. Review text is plain text; IDs are never shown. */\\r\\nexport function SupportingReviews({ citations }: { citations: readonly Citation[] }) {\\r\\n  const headingId = useId();\\r\\n  if (citations.length === 0) return null;\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <section aria-labelledby={headingId} className=\\\"space-y-2\\\">\\r\\n      <h4 id={headingId} className=\\\"font-medium\\\">\\r\\n        Supporting Reviews\\r\\n      </h4>\\r\\n      <ol className=\\\"space-y-3\\\" aria-label=\\\"Supporting Reviews\\\">\\r\\n        {citations.map(({ number, label, review }) => (\\r\\n          <li key={number} className=\\\"max-w-prose space-y-1 text-sm\\\">\\r\\n            <p>\\r\\n              <span className=\\\"font-medium\\\">{label}</span>{\\\" \\\"}\\r\\n              {review ? `${review.rating} out of 5` : \\\"Review not available\\\"}\\r\\n            </p>\\r\\n            {review && (\\r\\n              <>\\r\\n                <p className=\\\"whitespace-pre-wrap break-words\\\">{formatReviewText(review.text)}</p>\\r\\n                {review.reviewDate && (\\r\\n                  <p className=\\\"text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n                    <time dateTime={review.reviewDate}>{review.reviewDate.slice(0, 10)}</time>\\r\\n                  </p>\\r\\n                )}\\r\\n              </>\\r\\n            )}\\r\\n          </li>\\r\\n        ))}\\r\\n      </ol>\\r\\n    </section>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n---\\r\\ntitle: use client\\r\\ndescription: Learn how to use the use client directive to render a component on the client.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nThe `'use client'` directive declares an entry point for the components to be rendered on the **client side** and should be used when creating interactive user interfaces (UI) that require client-side JavaScript capabilities, such as state management, event handling, and access to browser APIs. This is a React feature.\\r\\n\\r\\n> **Good to know:**\\r\\n>\\r\\n> You do not need to add the `'use client'` directive to every file that contains Client Components. You only need to add it to the files whose components you want to render directly within Server Components. The `'use client'` directive defines the [server and client boundary](/docs/app/guides/server-and-client-boundary), and the components exported from such a file serve as entry points to the client.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Usage\\r\\n\\r\\nTo declare an entry point for the Client Components, add the `'use client'` directive **at the top of the file**, before any imports. The directive applies to the entire module and cannot be placed inside a component or function:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/components/counter.tsx\\\" highlight={1} switcher\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useState } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function Counter() {\\r\\n  const [count, setCount] = useState(0)\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <p>Count: {count}</p>\\r\\n      <button onClick={() => setCount(count + 1)}>Increment</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/components/counter.js\\\" highlight={1} switcher\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useState } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function Counter() {\\r\\n  const [count, setCount] = useState(0)\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <p>Count: {count}</p>\\r\\n      <button onClick={() => setCount(count + 1)}>Increment</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen using the `'use client'` directive, the props of the Client Components must be [serializable](https://react.dev/reference/rsc/use-client#serializable-types). This means the props need to be in a format that React can serialize when sending data from the server to the client.\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/components/counter.tsx\\\" highlight={4} switcher\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function Counter({\\r\\n  onClick /* âŒ Function is not serializable */,\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <button onClick={onClick}>Increment</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```jsx filename=\\\"app/components/counter.js\\\" highlight={4} switcher\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function Counter({\\r\\n  onClick /* âŒ Function is not serializable */,\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <button onClick={onClick}>Increment</button>\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Nesting Client Components within Server Components\\r\\n\\r\\nCombining Server and Client Components allows you to build applications that are both performant and interactive:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Server Components**: Use for static content, data fetching, and SEO-friendly elements.\\r\\n2. **Client Components**: Use for interactive elements that require state, effects, or browser APIs.\\r\\n# ReviewLens AI-Assisted Development and Review Standards\\r\\n\\r\\n**Project:** ReviewLens AI | **Team:** Fantastic Six | **Story:** S1-004 | **Related rules:** S1-BR-004, S1-BR-008, S1-BR-011\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core principle\\r\\n\\r\\nAI-generated code is treated as team-authored code. The developer who prompts, accepts, and commits it is its author and is fully responsible for it. It goes through the same pull request, review, testing, and Definition of Done as hand-written code.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. AI coding tools the team expects to use\\r\\n\\r\\n| Tool          | Typical use                                                                            |\\r\\n| ------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| Claude (chat) | Design questions, debugging, explaining errors, drafting docs                          |\\r\\n| ChatGPT       | Same as above; second opinion on generated code                                        |\\r\\n| Claude Code   | Agentic coding in the repo (scaffolding, config, tests, CI). Used in S1-005 and S1-007 |\\r\\n| OpenAI Codex  | Agentic coding in the repo. Used in S1-006                                             |\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Context supplied to AI tools\\r\\n\\r\\nContext we will provide (as relevant to the task):\\r\\n\\r\\n- The user story or issue text, including its acceptance criteria and the business rules it touches (for example S1-BR-004, S1-BR-008, S1-BR-011).\\r\\n- The tech stack: Next.js, React, TypeScript, Supabase PostgreSQL, Supabase Auth, Vercel.\\r\\n- The domain entities and relationships: User â†’ AnalysisTarget â†’ Reviews and IngestionAttempt.\\r\\n- The API model: Next.js route handlers that validate input and enforce authentication and ownership.\\r\\n- The specific files, types, and schema involved, not the whole repository.\\r\\n- Existing conventions: naming, folder structure, error response shape, validation approach.\\r\\n\\r\\nContext we will NOT provide:\\r\\n\\r\\n- API keys, service-role keys, database connection strings, tokens, or `.env` contents.\\r\\n- Real user data, personal information, or private review data that is not approved for sharing.\\r\\n- Credentials of any kind, even \\\"temporary\\\" ones.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a snippet contains a secret, redact it first. If a secret is accidentally pasted into an AI tool, treat it as leaked: rotate it immediately and tell the team.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Reviewing generated code\\r\\n\\r\\n- Before opening a pull request, the author must read and understand every line of generated code. Reviewers apply the same standard.\\r\\n- Understand it. If the author cannot explain a block, it does not get committed.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\"}"}]

### 2026-11-25T21:36:15.500Z — assistant (source record 13836)

S3-014 is issue #120. The app already resolves citations against the active target’s loaded reviews and displays supporting text, rating and date. I’ll build on that: keep a short inline evidence list and let users expand the full cited set when there are more reviews, clearly labeled as a subset of the complete dataset. This needs no new endpoint, schema or model call. S3-013 is still open; I’ll avoid its changed files and record the handoff for Neel.

That is\\r\\n\\r\\nName                                                                   LastWriteTime         Length\\r\\n----                                                                   -------------         ------\\r\\nrollout-2026-11-25T16-18-47-01a21493-4e13-7953-80d3-e343420d3024.jsonl 11/25/2026 4:18:47 PM 807385\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\"}"}]

className=\\\"space-y-2\\\">\\r\\n      <h4 id={headingId} className=\\\"font-medium\\\">\\r\\n        Supporting Reviews\\r\\n      </h4>\\r\\n      <p className=\\\"max-w-prose text-sm text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n        These reviews were cited in this answer. They are not the complete saved review dataset.\\r\\n      </p>\\r\\n      <SupportingReviewList\\r\\n        citations={citations.slice(0, INLINE_REVIEW_COUNT)}\\r\\n        label=\\\"Supporting Reviews\\\"\\r\\n      />\\r\\n      {hasMore && (\\r\\n        <details className=\\\"space-y-3 rounded-lg border border-border p-3\\\">\\r\\n          <summary className=\\\"cursor-pointer rounded-sm text-sm font-medium text-primary focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-ring\\\">\\r\\n            View all {citations.length} supporting reviews\\r\\n          </summary>\\r\\n          <p className=\\\"text-sm text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n            Showing all {citations.length} cited reviews in citation order. The first three are also\\r\\n            shown above.\\r\\n          </p>\\r\\n          <SupportingReviewList citations={citations} label=\\\"All supporting reviews\\\" />\\r\\n        </details>\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    </section>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction SupportingReviewList({\\r\\n  citations,\\r\\n  label,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  citations: readonly Citation[];\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <ol className=\\\"space-y-3\\\" aria-label={label}>\\r\\n      {citations.map(({ number, label, review }) => (\\r\\n        <li key={number} className=\\\"max-w-prose space-y-1 text-sm\\\">\\r\\n          <p>\\r\\n            <span className=\\\"font-medium\\\">{label}</span>{\\\" \\\"}\\r\\n            {review ? `${review.rating} out of 5` : \\\"Review not available\\\"}\\r\\n          </p>\\r\\n          {review && (\\r\\n            <>\\r\\n              <p className=\\\"whitespace-pre-wrap break-words\\\">{formatReviewText(review.text)}</p>\\r\\n              {review.reviewDate && (\\r\\n                <p className=\\\"text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n                  <time dateTime={review.reviewDate}>{review.reviewDate.slice(0, 10)}</time>\\r\\n                </p>\\r\\n              )}\\r\\n            </>\\r\\n          )}\\r\\n        </li>\\r\\n      ))}\\r\\n    </ol>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n M src/components/reviews/SupportingReviews.tsx\\n M tests/fixtures/auth-provider.mjs\\n?? tests/e2e/auth/supporting-reviews.spec.ts\\n?? tests/unit/components/reviews/ReviewQuestions.supporting-reviews.test.tsx\\n?? tests/unit/components/reviews/SupportingReviews.test.tsx\\n\"}"}]

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: <USER_HOME>/Documents/NJIT/Fall 26/CS490/CS490_Review_Lens_AI_Fantastic_Six/tests/e2e/auth/supporting-reviews.spec.ts\n@@\n     expect(await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= innerWidth)).toBe(true);\n+    await page.screenshot({\n+      path: `test-results/s3-014-supporting-reviews-${width}.png`,\n+      fullPage: true,\n+    });\n*** Add File: <USER_HOME>/Documents/NJIT/Fall 26/CS490/CS490_Review_Lens_AI_Fantastic_Six/docs/s3-014-supporting-reviews.md\n+# S3-014 — View Matching or Supporting Reviews\n+\n+Issue: #120. Author: Shawn. AI assistance: OpenAI Codex; original available dialogue and tool\n+records are retained in `ai-transcripts/2026-11-25_shawn_S3-014_codex.md`.\n+\n+## Selection contract and scope\n+\n+ReviewLens currently sends all authorized persisted reviews within its context limits. It has no\n+question-specific search/ranking layer. Therefore, this story's relevant set is the **unique review\n+IDs cited by the validated model response**, not every review supplied to the model and not a\n+claim that all potentially relevant reviews have been discovered.\n+\n+The workspace already resolves server-validated citation IDs against its active target's loaded\n+reviews. This implementation reuses that path and the existing `SupportingReviews` component:\n+\n+- Up to three numbered supporting reviews remain immediately visible beside the answer.\n+- When the cited set is larger, **View all N supporting reviews** expands the entire cited set in\n+  its existing citation order. The preview is repeated in that full list, as its explanation states.\n+- Every citation remains available; three is only an inline presentation limit, not an evidence\n+  selection or model limit. Answer quotations and their citation numbering remain unchanged.\n+- The section explicitly says these are cited reviews, **not the complete saved review dataset**.\n+- Stored review text, rating, and date are shown. Missing records retain their numbered\n+  **Review not available** slot; no missing text, date, or reviewer metadata is fabricated.\n+- The native disclosure supports Enter/Space, focus indication, and narrow layouts without a new\n+  dependency or dialog framework.\n+\n+The full saved-review browser belongs to S3-015. This story does not implement search, rating/date\n+filters, refresh, history, exports, or a new API. It does not change the model/provider/quota contract.\n+\n+## Data and security boundaries\n+\n+The backend still authenticates the user and checks target ownership before loading review data.\n+The response validator still checks citations and quotes against the authorized model context.\n+`buildCitationView` restricts client resolution to the active target; expanding a list cannot fetch\n+foreign or additional records. The explorer uses only resolved `Citation` objects and makes no\n+network request. Text remains escaped React text; the display-only `<br>` formatter is preserved.\n+Reviewer names/source metadata are not added to AI context or the supporting-evidence display.\n+\n+Existing `ReviewQuestions` cleanup removes the explorer with its answer on a new submission,\n+account/target transition, or Activity hide/restore. Non-answers do not show supporting evidence.\n+Exact quotation provenance does not prove semantic entailment; this UI does not add that guarantee.\n+\n+## Canonical rule mapping\n+\n+The canonical Sprint 3 rules were supplied in the task's instructor materials:\n+\n+| Rule | Implementation/evidence |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| S3-BR-020 | Full cited set can be inspected beyond the inline sample. |\n+| S3-BR-021 | Existing server checks and active-target citation resolution remain authoritative; integration tests reject foreign-target evidence. |\n+| S3-BR-022 | Displays stored text/rating/date; unavailable records are labeled, and markup is inert. |\n+| S3-BR-023 | Supporting-review expansion complements the already merged complete dataset browser; it does not replace it. |\n+| S3-BR-024 | Cited subset resolution uses active-target persisted reviews; no cross-target query or mutation is introduced. |\n+\n+## Integration handoff\n+\n+S3-013 PR #127 was still open when this work began. It hardens empty/missing-citation responses in\n+`ReviewQuestions.tsx` and `citations.ts`; this story leaves both files and its changed tests alone.\n+Its fail-closed invariant is compatible with this read-only presentation. Reconcile with its final\n+approved head before merge, and retain the response-validator, component, and security regressions.\n+\n+S3-015 PR #129 is already in the baseline. Its complete review list remains independent. Coordinate\n+future S3-016 filtering so a browsing filter does not silently replace or broaden an answer's cited\n+evidence. Persisted-history integration must retain stable target/review associations; it is outside\n+this story.\n+\n+## Verification and demo\n+\n+New component/integration tests cover preview/full-set boundaries, citation order, matching stored\n+fields, unavailable records, inert markup, uncited/foreign exclusion, and state cleanup. A dedicated\n+browser account owns seven fixture reviews, of which six are cited, making the distinction visible\n+without changing existing accounts' data. Browser tests use a mocked Q&A response and local fixture\n+provider; they do not certify hosted RLS or live-model behavior and make no paid calls.\n+\n+Manual happy path: open an owned target, obtain a grounded answer citing more than three reviews,\n+and expand **View all N supporting reviews**. Verify the later cited reviews and numbering. For\n+three or fewer citations, all are already inline and no expansion action is needed. A real model is\n+not guaranteed to cite more than three; use the deterministic browser case for reliable rehearsal.\n+\n+Failure/scope path: switch targets/accounts, start another question, or obtain a non-answer. Old\n+supporting evidence must disappear. A missing cited record must show unavailable without a broader\n+lookup. Inspect the separate complete saved-review list to distinguish uncited records.\n+\n+Final local check results will be recorded after validation. Public-deployment acceptance and\n+independent reviews remain separate from local deterministic verification.\n*** Update File: <USER_HOME>/Documents/NJIT/Fall 26/CS490/CS490_Review_Lens_AI_Fantastic_Six/docs/UI_UX_Standards.md\n@@\n-6. **Cards:**\n+6. **Cards:**\n*** End Patch"));

a pure move adds no lines                                                    |\\r\\n| New file no test loads                                      | Fails: it is in the report at 0% because `collectCoverageFrom` includes every source file                            |\\r\\n| New file containing only types/interfaces                   | Not gated: it emits no runtime code (detected with the TypeScript compiler)                                          |\\r\\n| Changed file missing from the coverage report               | **Error** (exit 2): the report is stale or from a partial `jest <path>` …748 tokens truncated…----------------------------------- |\\r\\n| `scripts/coverage/check-changed-coverage.ts` | Thin process entry point (argument/environment/output wiring only, no logic). The message says what to run; this never counts as a pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Requesting an exclusion\\r\\n\\r\\nExclusions are for code that cannot reasonably be unit-tested in Jest **and** has no real logic:\\r\\nfor example, a thin Next.js `loading.tsx` that is verified by Playwright.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Branch: `s3-001`. Author: melissa. AI assistance: OpenAI Codex.\\r\\n\\r\\nCreating a target saves an analysis with the user-chosen target name. The form now\\r\\nexplains this behavior. The protected application rail lists saved analyses by that\\r\\nname and opens the existing workspace. Names are independent of source URLs.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Persistence and ownership\\r\\n\\r\\nThe existing `analysis_targets` row is the saved analysis identity. No duplicate\\r\\nanalysis table, migration, or separate save operation is needed for this story:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Required value          | Existing persistence                                           |\\r\\n| ----------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| Analysis name           | `analysis_targets.name`, chosen in TargetForm                  |\\r\\n| Owner                   | `analysis_targets.owner_id`, derived from the verified session |\\r\\n| Source reference        | `analysis_targets.source_url`                                  |\\r\\n| AnalysisTarget identity | `analysis_targets.id`                                          |\\r\\n| Current review dataset  | `reviews.analysis_target_id` references that identity          |\\r\\n\\r\\nNew analyses start with an empty dataset. Ingestion adds persisted reviews to the\\r\\nsame target. Sidebar navigation writes neither the source nor reviews. Existing\\r\\ntargets appear automatically, and names remain subject to the shared client/server\\r\\nvalidation: visible nonempty text, no unsafe controls, at most 120 characters.\\r\\nThe name field is independent of the URL field. Post-save rename is S3-002.\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery AppShell caller passes the server-verified user ID. The shell uses the existing\\r\\nsession-scoped Supabase client and owner-filtered list service, with database RLS\\r\\nas an additional boundary. Only IDs and names reach sidebar navigation. The list\\r\\ninherits the existing newest-first, 200-target bound; My Targets remains available.\\r\\nLoad failures preserve workspace content and offer a retry without database details.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Functionality and security review\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pre-PR review reproduced a stale sidebar after saving a second analysis and\\r\\nusing browser Back to revisit the first workspace. API-based creation did not\\r\\ninvalidate Next's client history cache. TargetForm now refreshes the client router\\r\\nafter replacing the route with the saved workspace. A production browser regression\\r\\nverifies that both saved names remain available after Back. Unit tests verify that\\r\\nrefresh follows successful navigation and does not run for failed or stale saves.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe review traced every protected shell caller to its verified session guard,\\r\\nchecked the owner-filtered repository queries and existing RLS policies, and checked\\r\\nthat source URLs and review associations remain unchanged. Names render as escaped\\r\\nReact text, sidebar links use encoded target IDs, and load failures omit database\\r\\ndetails. A browser regression verifies sign-out and Back show sign-in without an\\r\\naccessible sidebar, then checks both the HTTP response and rendered workspace deny\\r\\na different user's target name and review data. The sign-out visibility assertion\\r\\naccounts for Next's hidden inactive Activity route trees; it does not claim to erase\\r\\npreviously downloaded browser memory.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Validation\\r\\n\\r\\n- Unit tests cover verified-owner wiring, minimal sidebar data, active links,\\r\\n  duplicate names, escaped text, empty lists, and recoverable failures.\\r\\n- Existing API/repository tests cover authenticated saving, rejected invalid names,\\r\\n  forged owner arguments, source persistence, and owner-filtered listing.\\r\\n- Browser tests in `tests/e2e/auth/saved-analyses.spec.ts` cover choosing a name\\r\\n  unrelated to the URL, trimming, a fresh login, refresh, same workspace identity,\\r\\n  persisted reviews, cross-user sidebar isolation, a 375px viewport, saving followed\\r\\n  by Back navigation, sign-out, and unauthorized direct workspace requests.\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal check results are recorded below after execution. No push, remote migration,\\r\\ndeployment, or CI run is performed by this task.\\r\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"7f6428\",\"wall_time_seconds\":5.0152612,\"session_id\":98163,\"original_token_count\":0,\"output\":\"\"}"}]

The current production server cannot emit that shape, but accepting it at the client boundary could allow a future regression or unexpected response to render a purported grounded answer without evidence.\\n\\n## Changes\\n\\n- Fail closed when an answered browser response has no citation.\\n- Add a second UI guard so a malformed answered result cannot render without evidence even if the API helper is bypassed.\\n- Update deterministic answered fixtures to cite the active target's real test Review identity.\\n- Add/strengthen API, component, target-switching, and Playwright evidence assertions.\\n- Document the inherited evidence flow, the narrow S3-013 gap, and security verification.\\n- Preserve PR #106's safe display-only review line-break formatting.\\n\\nNo prompt, model, quota, database schema, migration, dependency, evidence-count limit, ingestion, or persistence behavior changed.\\n\\n## Acceptance criteria and rule traceability\\n\\n- **Grounded answers show supporting evidence:** existing multi-review component coverage plus the strengthened answered-response invariant.\\n- **Evidence comes from the active persisted dataset:** existing server ownership/context checks and client active-target resolution remain unchanged; production target-switching fixtures now cite the actual loaded target Review.\\n- **Stable identity is retained:** Review UUIDs remain server-validated and travel to the client only for resolution; the UI shows numbered labels rather than raw IDs.\\n- **No fabricated quotation or metadata:** existing exact quote-in-review validation remains; displayed text/rating/date come from persisted Review objects; reviewer names remain excluded.\\n- **Invalid/unavailable evidence is safe:** unknown/foreign IDs fail closed server-side; client resolution never broadens beyond active-target reviews.\\n- **Non-answers show no misleading evidence:** insufficient-evidence, scope refusal, and failures remain citation-free.\\n\\nIssue #119 references S3-BR-020 through S3-BR-022. Their canonical definitions are not present in the repository; this PR traces to the issue text without inventing those definitions. The issue's two-to-five excerpt guidance is treated as reasonable rather than a hard cap, so evidence needed by a claim is not truncated.\\n\\n## Security and ownership\\n\\n- Identity still comes from the verified session, never a client-supplied user ID.\\n- Ownership is checked before persisted reviews are loaded.\\n- Citation membership is checked against the exact authorized context sent to the model.\\n- Client resolution filters by the active target and never queries for unrelated reviews.\\n- Review text remains an escaped React text child; no raw-HTML rendering was introduced.\\n- No secrets, real private data, live OpenAI calls, Outscraper calls, or service-role access were used.\\n\\nBecause this touches the AI-data boundary and untrusted review display, please review the fail-closed citation invariant, target-scoped resolution, and plain-text rendering specifically.\\n\\n## Automated validation\\n\\nPassed locally:\\n\\n- `npm run format:check`\\n- `npm run check:migrations`\\n- `npm run lint`\\n- `npm run typecheck`\\n- focused S3-013 run: 6 suites / 117 tests\\n- corrected production target-switching suite: 9/9 tests\\n- `npm test`: 107 suites / 2,034 tests\\n- `npm run build`: Next.js 16.4.6 production build; 12/12 static pages\\n- full coverage: 95.55% lines (9,803/10,259), 93.39% branches, 94.9% functions\\n- changed-code coverage: 100% (7/7 source lines), 8/8 changed branch outcomes\\n\\nEnvironment-limited checks:\\n\\n- Authenticated Playwright: 3/5 review-question scenarios passed. tests/unit/components/reviews/SupportingReviews.test.tsx\\nfeature/S3-014-supporting-reviews\\n24f083fb S3-015: Verify and complete persisted review dataset browsing (#129)\\nimport { useId } from \\\"react\\\";\\r\\nimport type { Citation } from \\\"@/lib/qa/citations\\\";\\r\\nimport { formatReviewText } from \\\"@/lib/reviews/format-review-text\\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nconst INLINE_REVIEW_COUNT = 3;\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Numbered cited evidence, with access to the full cited set (S3-014, issue #120). */\\r\\nexport function SupportingReviews({ citations }: { citations: readonly Citation[] }) {\\r\\n  const headingId = useId();\\r\\n  if (citations.length === 0) return null;\\r\\n  const hasMore = citations.length > INLINE_REVIEW_COUNT;\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <section aria-labelledby={headingId} className=\\\"space-y-2\\\">\\r\\n      <h4 id={headingId} className=\\\"font-medium\\\">\\r\\n        Supporting Reviews\\r\\n      </h4>\\r\\n      <p className=\\\"max-w-prose text-sm text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n        These reviews were cited in this answer. The first three are also\\r\\n            shown above.\\r\\n          </p>\\r\\n          <SupportingReviewList citations={citations} label=\\\"All supporting reviews\\\" />\\r\\n        </details>\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    </section>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction SupportingReviewList({\\r\\n  citations,\\r\\n  label,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  citations: readonly Citation[];\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <ol className=\\\"space-y-3\\\" aria-label={label}>\\r\\n      {citations.map(({ number, label, review }) => (\\r\\n        <li key={number} className=\\\"max-w-prose space-y-1 text-sm\\\">\\r\\n          <p>\\r\\n            <span className=\\\"font-medium\\\">{label}</span>{\\\" \\\"}\\r\\n            {review ? `${review.rating} out of 5` : \\\"Review not available\\\"}\\r\\n          </p>\\r\\n          {review && (\\r\\n            <>\\r\\n              <p className=\\\"whitespace-pre-wrap break-words\\\">{formatReviewText(review.text)}</p>\\r\\n              {review.reviewDate && (\\r\\n                <p className=\\\"text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n                  <time dateTime={review.reviewDate}>{review.reviewDate.slice(0, 10)}</time>\\r\\n                </p>\\r\\n              )}\\r\\n            </>\\r\\n          )}\\r\\n        </li>\\r\\n      ))}\\r\\n    </ol>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n# S3-014 â€” View Matching or Supporting Reviews\\r\\n\\r\\nIssue: #120. Author: Shawn. AI assistance: OpenAI Codex; original available dialogue and tool\\r\\nrecords are retained in `ai-transcripts/2026-11-25_shawn_S3-014_codex.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Selection contract and scope\\r\\n\\r\\nReviewLens currently sends all authorized persisted reviews within its context limits. It has no\\r\\nquestion-specific search/ranking layer. Therefore, this story's relevant set is the **unique review\\r\\nIDs cited by the validated model response**, not every review supplied to the model and not a\\r\\nclaim that all potentially relevant reviews have been discovered.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe workspace already resolves server-validated citation IDs against its active target's loaded\\r\\nreviews. This implementation reuses that path and the existing `SupportingReviews` component:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Up to three numbered supporting reviews remain immediately visible beside the answer.\\r\\n- When the cited set is larger, **View all N supporting reviews** expands the entire cited set in\\r\\n  its existing citation order. The preview is repeated in that full list, as its explanation states.\\r\\n- Every citation remains available; three is only an inline presentation limit, not an evidence\\r\\n  selection or model limit. Answer quotations and their citation numbering remain unchanged.\\r\\n- The section explicitly says these are cited reviews, **not the complete saved review dataset**.\\r\\n- Stored review text, rating, and date are shown. Missing records retain their numbered\\r\\n  **Review not available** slot; no missing text, date, or reviewer metadata is fabricated.\\r\\n- The native disclosure supports Enter/Space, focus indication, and narrow layouts without a new\\r\\n  dependency or dialog framework.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe full saved-review browser belongs to S3-015. This story does not implement search, rating/date\\r\\nfilters, refresh, history, exports, or a new API. It does not change the model/provider/quota contract.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Data and security boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\nThe backend still authenticates the user and checks target ownership before loading review data.\\r\\nThe response validator still checks citations and quotes against the authorized model context.\\r\\n`buildCitationView` restricts client resolution to the active target; expanding a list cannot fetch\\r\\nforeign or additional records. The explorer uses only resolved `Citation` objects and makes no\\r\\nnetwork request. Text remains escaped React text; the display-only `<br>` formatter is preserved.\\r\\nReviewer names/source metadata are not added to AI context or the supporting-evidence display.\\r\\n\\r\\nExisting `ReviewQuestions` cleanup removes the explorer with its answer on a new submission,\\r\\naccount/target transition, or Activity hide/restore. Non-answers do not show supporting evidence.\\r\\nExact quotation provenance does not prove semantic entailment; this UI does not add that guarantee.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Canonical rule mapping\\r\\n\\r\\nThe canonical Sprint 3 rules were supplied in the task's instructor materials:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Rule      | Implementation/evidence                                                                                                              |\\r\\n| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\\r\\n| S3-BR-020 | Full cited set can be inspected beyond the inline sample.                                                                            |\\r\\n| S3-BR-021 | Existing server checks and active-target citation resolution remain authoritative; integration tests reject foreign-target evidence. |\\r\\n| S3-BR-022 | Displays stored text/rating/date; unavailable records are labeled, and markup is inert.                                              |\\r\\n| S3-BR-023 | Supporting-review expansion complements the already merged complete dataset browser; it does not replace it.                         |\\r\\n| S3-BR-024 | Cited subset resolution uses active-target persisted reviews; no cross-target query or mutation is introduced.                       |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Integration handoff\\r\\n\\r\\nS3-013 PR #127 was still open when this work began. It hardens empty/missing-citation responses in\\r\\n`ReviewQuestions.tsx` and `citations.ts`; this story leaves both files and its changed tests alone.\\r\\nIts fail-closed invariant is compatible with this read-only presentation. Reconcile with its final\\r\\napproved head before merge, and retain the response-validator, component, and security regressions.\\r\\n\\r\\nS3-015 PR #129 is already in the baseline. Its complete review list remains independent. Coordinate\\r\\nfuture S3-016 filtering so a browsing filter does not silently replace or broaden an answer's cited\\r\\nevidence. Persisted-history integration must retain stable target/review associations; it is outside\\r\\nthis story.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification and demo\\r\\n\\r\\nNew component/integration tests cover preview/full-set boundaries, citation order, matching stored\\r\\nfields, unavailable records, inert markup, uncited/foreign exclusion, and state cleanup. A dedicated\\r\\nbrowser account owns seven fixture reviews, of which six are cited, making the distinction visible\\r\\nwithout changing existing accounts' data. Browser tests use a mocked Q&A response and local fixture\\r\\nprovider; they do not certify hosted RLS or live-model behavior and make no paid calls.\\r\\n\\r\\nManual happy path: open an owned target, obtain a grounded answer citing more than three reviews,\\r\\nand expand **View all N supporting reviews**. Verify the later cited reviews and numbering. For\\r\\nthree or fewer citations, all are already inline and no expansion action is needed. A real model is\\r\\nnot guaranteed to cite more than three; use the deterministic browser case for reliable rehearsal.\\r\\n\\r\\nFailure/scope path: switch targets/accounts, start another question, or obtain a non-answer. Old\\r\\nsupporting evidence must disappear. A missing cited record must show unavailable without a broader\\r\\nlookup. Inspect the separate complete saved-review list to distinguish uncited records.\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal verification on the `24f083fb` main baseline:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Focused component/integration and inherited citation suites: 24/24 passed.\\r\\n- Full Jest with coverage: 112 suites / 2,052 tests passed; no skipped tests.\\r\\n- Line/statement coverage: 96.10%; branches: 93.67%; functions: 95.47%.\\r\\n- Changed-line coverage: 100% (54/54 source lines); 4/4 changed branch outcomes covered.\\r\\n- Formatting, ESLint, explicit TypeScript checks, migration filename checks and production build\\r\\n  passed. Build generated all 12 static pages.\\r\\n- Dedicated browser case passed in development and production, including keyboard open/close,\\r\\n  375px layout, citation-only membership and reload cleanup. Desktop/mobile screenshots remain\\r\\n  outside the source tree in the isolated checkout's ignored `test-results` folder.\\r\\n- Full production authentication browser suite: 42 passed / 2 failed. The failures are the existing\\r\\n  target-creation cases at `analysis-targets.spec.ts:99` and `:177`, which expect the old\\r\\n  `Target created. You can now start ingestion.` text. Their snapshots show successful workspace\\r\\n  navigation. Both failures were independently reproduced on a separate unchanged-main\\r\\n  `24f083fb` production build without the S3-014 source or fixture changes. They predate this story\\r\\n  and remain unchanged here.\\r\\n- Temporary combination with S3-013 `fa106e14` plus current main: the 11 new tests passed. Three\\r\\n  mutations were caught: truncating the expanded set (5 test failures), omitting the explorer\\r\\n  (9 failures), and removing active-target resolution (1 failure). All mutations were restored.\\r\\n\\r\\nNo PR/remote CI or public-deployment verification is claimed. Independent review and applicable\\r\\ndeployed acceptance remain separate from local deterministic checks. Before merging S3-013's\\r\\nstricter validator, its owner must also reconcile existing browser stubs that still omit\\r\\n`citedReviewIds` (for example, `stale-review-context.spec.ts`); the new S3-014 fixture already returns\\r\\nthe explicit current citation contract.\\r\\n\"}"}]

**Yes—the current implementation meets S3-014’s functional requirements locally.** I rechecked issue #120, the code and tests, and confirmed the files match the validated copies.

| Requirement | Implementation |
|---|---|
| Inspect the larger relevant review set | “View all N supporting reviews” exposes every cited review beyond the three-item preview. |
| Fit our Q&A strategy | Uses validated model citations—the issue explicitly allows this approach. |
| Distinguish from the complete dataset | Explicit explanatory text; uncited reviews are excluded. |
| Preserve evidence identity and accuracy | Retains citation order and stored text/rating/date; missing records show “Review not available.” |
| Maintain isolation | Reuses active-target resolution; foreign evidence is excluded and old evidence clears on account/target changes. |

Verification includes **2,052 passing Jest tests**, desktop/mobile browser coverage, **100% changed-line coverage**, and three mutations caught by the tests. Formatting, lint, typecheck and build passed.

**It is not fully Done yet:** PR review, current-head CI and applicable public-deployment acceptance remain. The broader browser suite also has two independently confirmed, pre-existing main failures involving old target-creation banner expectations.

No additional S3-014 implementation gap was identified in this check.
